# Audit report — metric-natural reciprocal/screen split selectors

Date: 2026-08-04

Grade: `VERIFIED_WITH_CAVEATS_BOUNDED_METRIC_NATURAL_SELECTOR_ATLAS`

## Result first

The complete metric can select a reciprocal clock/ruler plane on some broken-symmetry strata, but
it cannot select one unique smooth plane on the full retained metric domain.

The obstruction is exact on the registered round complete `R x S3` control. Its metric selects the
ultrastatic time line, while spatial `SO(3)` symmetry selects no ruler line. The full commutant
calculation admits invariant projector ranks only

```text
0, 1, 3, 4,
```

not the rank two required by a Lorentzian clock/ruler plane. This rules out one universal unique
metric-natural split on any retained domain containing that branch. It does not prove that a future
native law cannot exclude or add structure to the branch.

The positive side is equally exact. A metric-self-adjoint curvature operator with distinct causal
simple eigendirections gives a polynomial rank-two projector. The construction is idempotent,
metric-self-adjoint, and equivariant under an exact rational Lorentz boost. The squashed complete
`S3` control realizes this geometry conditionally: ultrastatic time plus its unoriented simple
Ricci/Hopf line. In the reordered homogeneous frame its Ricci spectrum is
`(0,1/2,3/2,3/2)`, and the exact algebraic control is `A=2I+2 Ric#`. The branch is off shell and
does not select physics.

The selector cannot be continued uniquely through the round limit. Two simple-spectrum families,
related by the round spatial isometry rotating the squashing axis, reach the same round curvature
operator with different limiting rank-two projectors. At the collision, the natural object is the
two-dimensional orbit of all unoriented spatial lines, not one member.

The frozen intrinsic two-form family supplies a second branch-local positive result: six candidates
have an exact rank-two kernel on their nonzero loci. The same construction becomes rank four on its
zero graph and fails unique projective continuation on three great circles. The zero, blocked, and
degenerate controls remain in the census.

## The three architectures are now distinct

1. **Query bundle:** every ordered observer/ruler query carries a tautological reciprocal plane on
   the total pair-frame bundle. This is already derived and does not itself derive a natural
   spacetime section; smooth sections can exist without being selected.
2. **Branch-derived split:** curvature, Hessian, symmetry, holonomy, or intrinsic forms can derive a
   plane where their spectra/ranks are regular. These are stratified and can fail at ties or zeros.
3. **Realized universal field:** one smooth `N` over spacetime would require an additional rule or a
   domain that removes the certified symmetry/defect obstructions. It is not currently derived.

A chosen coframe's first two legs constitute a fourth, conditional input architecture. They are not
metric-natural if local Lorentz-related coframes represent the same metric.

## Why this is not another generic underdetermination

The audit gives a positive mechanism, a negative theorem, and their exact join:

```text
broken symmetry / simple spectrum / nonzero intrinsic form
    -> branch-local natural plane;

round symmetry / eigenvalue collision / zero or rank change
    -> no unique smooth member;

all observer queries
    -> tautological total-space plane, no natural spacetime section selected.
```

The missing question is no longer merely “which plane?” It is whether UDT's still-open laws need a
single spacetime plane at all, or can be formulated on the query bundle and descend without one.

## Complete candidate disposition

`SELECTOR_OUTCOMES.tsv` classifies all 18 preregistered routes. It retains zero-jet metric data,
coframe presentation, query data, `phi` jets, Ricci and Riemann/Weyl spectra, scalar gradients,
Killing symmetry, holonomy, intrinsic forms, round and squashed complete controls, whole-solution
and boundary/topology operations, set-valued outputs, rank-changing atlases, and excluded
action/dynamical selection.

Riemann/Weyl, joint-gradient, Killing, and holonomy routes are conditional capabilities, not frozen
complete witnesses. The round isometry obstruction reaches every unique metric-natural nonlocal or
whole-solution construction on that exact control; positive nonlocal capability on less-symmetric
branches remains unclassified. Boundary/topology routes may decorate or change the domain but have
no registered selector operation. Action/dynamical selection remains downstream and excluded.

# Pair-space metric-transform and SNe-readout audit

Date: 2026-07-24

Base: `148f84ea467d9716f8706c161efd45d0a381ecaa`

Preregistration: `9f34e799388d7b59539f95fc666d0d7aaed2f778`

Clerical source-hash correction:
`9d95652c70a2e43adcd80e3291aa038a2afef27f`

Grade: **VERIFIED-WITH-CAVEATS**

## Result

The three exact bounded profiles already found in the repository all define
valid bounded metric transforms when they are composed with an independently
supplied base metric:

- projective `tanh`;
- WR-L proper exponential `1-exp(-s)`;
- conditional round B19 `(2/pi)atan(sinh(2s))`.

This is a genuine structural result. A finite, uncrossed maximum pair distance
does not require a preferred center. It can arise by applying a saturating
metric-preserving transform to an unbounded base observer distance.

It is not yet a physical `X_max` derivation. All three profiles pass, and the
base metric, event pairing, profile, `kappa`, and scale `X` remain unselected.

## The important SNe clarification

The SNe success does **not** belong to the WR-L proper-distance exponential.
It belongs to a different assembly of readouts from the same WR-L lapse:

```text
clock:          1+z=exp(phi)
areal radius:   r/X=1-exp(-2phi)
optics:         d_L=(1+z)^2 r
```

Together these give `d_L/X=z(z+2)`.

The observed full-covariance replay is:

```text
WR-L areal/optical      chi2/dof 0.9098574003059212, RMS 0.15816422433950028
projective areal J1     chi2/dof 2.166501637078457,  RMS 0.3074025916996164
```

Thus the data favor the complete registered WR-L clock/areal/optical assembly
over the complete registered projective J1 assembly. They do not choose among
the abstract pair-metric transforms.

This gives precise content to the suggestion that several effects overlap:
the clock, radial length, areal radius, and optical distance are distinct
faces of one metric. Combining the correct faces is physically load-bearing;
counting one face twice or substituting proper distance for areal distance
changes the prediction.

## Candidate disposition

- `WRL_AREAL_OPTICAL`: observed near the SNe data under its registered
  conditional static premises.
- `PROJECTIVE_AREAL_J1`: observed shape failure under its registered complete
  comparator premises.
- `WRL_PROPER_PAIR_TRANSFORM`: valid conditional pair metric; not an SNe law
  without a new optical identification.
- `B19_ROUND`: valid conditional pair transform; no clock/redshift/optical
  solder and therefore not scored.
- `FC12`: free profile; compatible by choice, not selected.
- conditional temporal-phi separation family: supplies a possible base
  observer distance only on its stated branch; full cosmological readout open.
- retired `P_ell`: excluded.

## What is learned

1. Bounded pair distance is mathematically compatible with no preferred
   center.
2. Metric-transform validity does not select the positional law.
3. Saturated pair comparison is generally nonlocal and is not ordinary
   integrated path length.
4. The strong SNe clue is branch-assembly-specific: it points to WR-L's
   joined clock, areal, and optical readouts.
5. BAO, CMB anisotropy, the CMB dipole, and black-hole behavior are not
   consequences of this result. They remain future independent tests if a
   global branch is derived.

## Remaining gate

The smallest missing object is no longer “some bounded formula.” It is a
metric-derived, center-free two-observer optical construction that determines
in one structure:

- which events on two observer histories are compared;
- the base observer-rest separation;
- clock comparison;
- angular/area transport and cut-locus behavior;
- whether its supremum is the physical `X_max`.

The WR-L SNe result is a strong calibration clue for that construction, but
must not be used to impose it.

## Authority boundary

No universal profile, global `X_max`, BAO/CMB/black-hole explanation, action,
source, carrier, boundary, density response, or mass law is derived here. No
GPU work, canonization, or repository reorganization was performed.

# Exact pair-space derivation

## 1. Conditional metric-transform theorem

Let `(M,d)` be a metric space. Let `X>0`, `kappa>0`, and let
`f:[0,infinity)->[0,1)` obey:

- `f(0)=0`;
- `f(s)>0` for `s>0`;
- `f` is increasing;
- `f` is concave.

Define

```text
D_f(p,q) = X f(kappa d(p,q)).
```

Then `D_f` is a metric.

Nonnegativity, symmetry, and identity of indiscernibles follow immediately
from those properties of `d`, positivity of `X,kappa`, and strict positivity
of `f` away from zero.

For `x,y>=0`, concavity and `f(0)=0` give

```text
f(x) >= x/(x+y) f(x+y),
f(y) >= y/(x+y) f(x+y).
```

Adding yields subadditivity:

```text
f(x+y) <= f(x)+f(y).
```

Consequently,

```text
d(p,q) <= d(p,r)+d(r,q)

=> f(kappa d(p,q))
   <= f(kappa[d(p,r)+d(r,q)])
   <= f(kappa d(p,r))+f(kappa d(r,q)).
```

This is the triangle inequality for `D_f`.

This theorem is mathematical and conditional. It does not derive the base
observer metric, event pairing, `X`, `kappa`, or the physical profile.

## 2. The three registered transforms

The registered normalized profiles are

```text
f_T(s) = tanh(s),
f_E(s) = 1-exp(-s),
f_R(s) = (2/pi) atan(sinh(2s)).
```

Their derivatives are

```text
f_T'(s)  = sech(s)^2,
f_T''(s) = -2 sech(s)^2 tanh(s);

f_E'(s)  = exp(-s),
f_E''(s) = -exp(-s);

f_R'(s)  = (4/pi) sech(2s),
f_R''(s) = -(8/pi) sech(2s) tanh(2s).
```

All three vanish at zero, are strictly increasing for finite nonnegative
`s`, are strictly concave for `s>0`, and approach one from below. Each
therefore satisfies the theorem.

If the base metric has unbounded diameter, then

```text
sup D_f = X,
```

and `X` is not attained at any finite base distance. If the base diameter is
the finite supremum `Delta`, continuity gives

```text
sup D_f = X f(kappa Delta) < X.
```

The first statement is an exact way to construct an uncrossed finite
pair-distance supremum without a preferred center. It is not a derivation of
physical `X_max`, because the base metric, event pairing, profile, and scale
remain unselected.

## 3. These are not ordinary local lengths

For strictly concave `f`, `s>0`, and integer `n>1`,

```text
n f(s/n) > f(s).
```

Therefore, subdividing a base geodesic and summing transformed segment
distances gives more than the transformed endpoint distance. `D_f` is in
general a nonlocal pair comparison, not the length generated by integrating
a local line element.

This is important for the co-present interpretation: a bounded observer-pair
comparison can be mathematically coherent without being a local signal path.
It still does not establish that this is UDT's physical comparison law.

## 4. Collinear composition

When the supplied base depth is additive along an ordered collinear chain,
the normalized transformed distance `y=f(s)` composes associatively.

Projective:

```text
y12 = (y1+y2)/(1+y1 y2),
inverse depth = atanh(y).
```

WR-L proper exponential:

```text
y12 = y1+y2-y1 y2,
inverse depth = -log(1-y).
```

Round B19:

```text
a_i = tan(pi y_i/2),
a12 = a1 sqrt(1+a2^2)+a2 sqrt(1+a1^2),
y12 = (2/pi) atan(a12),
inverse depth = (1/2)asinh(tan(pi y/2)).
```

Associativity is inherited by conjugating ordinary addition with each
profile and its inverse. For arbitrary triples, this scalar rule does not
replace angular transport, and the cut locus can still make path transport
multivalued.

## 5. WR-L contains several distinct readouts

On the registered conditional WR-L branch,

```text
A = exp(-2phi) = 1-r/X.
```

The same lapse gives:

```text
clock/redshift:            1+z = exp(phi),
areal radius:              r/X = 1-exp(-2phi),
proper radial distance:    D_prop/(2X) = 1-exp(-phi).
```

These are not interchangeable. The full registered optical relation is

```text
d_L = (1+z)^2 D_A,   D_A=r,
```

so

```text
d_L/X
 = exp(2phi)[1-exp(-2phi)]
 = exp(2phi)-1
 = (1+z)^2-1
 = z(z+2).
```

The successful SNe score therefore tests the assembly

```text
WR-L clock + WR-L areal radius + registered static optics,
```

not the WR-L proper-distance profile and not a universal pair-distance
operator.

For the registered projective J1 comparator,

```text
r/X = tanh(phi),
u = 1+z = exp(phi),
d_L/X = u^2 (u^2-1)/(u^2+1).
```

This is likewise a complete conditional readout and can be scored. The B19,
FC12, temporal-phi, and bare pair-transform candidates do not yet have the
complete clock/areal/optical join needed for an SNe score.

## 6. Exact observational replay

With the preregistered 1,580-object Pantheon+SH0ES cut, full STAT+SYS
covariance, and one additive magnitude offset:

```text
WR-L areal/optical:
  chi2/dof = 0.9098574003059212
  RMS      = 0.15816422433950028 mag

projective areal J1:
  chi2/dof = 2.166501637078457
  RMS      = 0.3074025916996164 mag
```

A separate NumPy direct solve reproduces these values within the
preregistered tolerances.

Observation: the registered WR-L areal/optical assembly is strongly preferred
to the registered projective J1 assembly on this SNe slice.

It does not follow that the exponential proper-distance transform has been
selected. Nor do the unscored candidates fail. Their necessary readout joins
remain open.

    64	   data.

   100	For strictly concave `f`, `s>0`, and integer `n>1`,
   101	
   102	```text
   103	n f(s/n) > f(s).
   104	```
   105	
   106	Therefore, subdividing a base geodesic and summing transformed segment
   107	distances gives more than the transformed endpoint distance. `D_f` is in
   108	general a nonlocal pair comparison, not the length generated by integrating
   109	a local line element.
   110	
   111	This is important for the co-present interpretation: a bounded observer-pair
   112	comparison can be mathematically coherent without being a local signal path.
   113	It still does not establish that this is UDT's physical comparison law.

   115	## 4. Collinear composition
   116	
   117	When the supplied base depth is additive along an ordered collinear chain,
   118	the normalized transformed distance `y=f(s)` composes associatively.
   119	
   120	Projective:
   121	
   122	```text
   123	y12 = (y1+y2)/(1+y1 y2),
   124	inverse depth = atanh(y).
   125	```
   126	
   127	WR-L proper exponential:
   128	
   129	```text
   130	y12 = y1+y2-y1 y2,
   131	inverse depth = -log(1-y).
   132	```
   133	
   134	Round B19:
   135	
   136	```text
   137	a_i = tan(pi y_i/2),
   138	a12 = a1 sqrt(1+a2^2)+a2 sqrt(1+a1^2),
   139	y12 = (2/pi) atan(a12),
   140	inverse depth = (1/2)asinh(tan(pi y/2)).
   141	```
   142	
   143	Associativity is inherited by conjugating ordinary addition with each
   144	profile and its inverse. For arbitrary triples, this scalar rule does not
   145	replace angular transport, and the cut locus can still make path transport
   146	multivalued.

   148	## 5. WR-L contains several distinct readouts
   149	
   150	On the registered conditional WR-L branch,
   151

   154	```
   155	
   156	The same lapse gives:
   157	
   158	```text
   159	clock/redshift:            1+z = exp(phi),
   160	areal radius:              r/X = 1-exp(-2phi),
   161	proper radial distance:    D_prop/(2X) = 1-exp(-phi).
   162	```
   163	
   164	These are not interchangeable. The full registered optical relation is
   165	
   166	```text
   167	d_L = (1+z)^2 D_A,   D_A=r,
   168	```
   169	
   170	so
   171	
   172	```text
   173	d_L/X
   174	 = exp(2phi)[1-exp(-2phi)]
   175	 = exp(2phi)-1
   176	 = (1+z)^2-1
   177	 = z(z+2).
   178	```
   179	
   180	The successful SNe score therefore tests the assembly
   181	
   182	```text
   183	WR-L clock + WR-L areal radius + registered static optics,
   184	```
   185	
   186	not the WR-L proper-distance profile and not a universal pair-distance
   187	operator.
   188	
   189	For the registered projective J1 comparator,
   190	
   191	```text
   192	r/X = tanh(phi),
   193	u = 1+z = exp(phi),
   194	d_L/X = u^2 (u^2-1)/(u^2+1).
   195	```
   196	
   197	This is likewise a complete conditional readout and can be scored. The B19,
   198	FC12, temporal-phi, and bare pair-transform candidates do not yet have the
   199	complete clock/areal/optical join needed for an SNe score.

    18	## 2. What the founding derivation actually types
    19	
    20	The original derivation begins with a positive reciprocal comparison at supplied relative depth
    21	`delta`:
    22	
    23	```text
    24	S(delta)=diag(exp(-delta),exp(delta)).
    25	```
    26	
    27	The registered relation laws give
    28	
    29	```text
    30	S(delta_1) S(delta_2)=S(delta_1+delta_2),
    31	S(-delta)=S(delta)^-1,
    32	S(0)=I.
    33	```
    34	
    35	These equations type an ordered relational comparison. Source and target roles reverse, a matched
    36	intermediate comparison composes, and self-comparison is neutral. The operator does not require a
    37	metric path once `delta` has been supplied.
    38

    40	
    41	```text

    47	
    48	## 3. Why “through an intermediate position” is not an endpoint-only theorem
    49	
    50	The strongest endpoint reading uses the source sentence that comparisons compose through an
    51	intermediate position. In its derivation context, that statement produces the multiplicative
    52	Cauchy equation on an already supplied additive scalar:
    53	
    54	```text
    55	g(delta_AB) g(delta_BC)=g(delta_AC).
    56	```
    57	
    58	It establishes consistency of the abstract character. It contains no variable for a spacetime path,
    59	homotopy class, parallel transport, endpoint frame, or complete angular data. It therefore cannot
    60	compare two distinct metric paths with the same endpoints.
    61	
    62	The exact factorization
    63	
    64	```text
    65	U(p,q)=S(phi(p))^-1 S(phi(q))
    66	```
    67	
    68	is a legitimate endpoint-only realization only after a common global reciprocal section and the
    69	physical identification `delta(p,q)=phi(q)-phi(p)` are supplied. The current source explicitly keeps
    70	that join open and notes that generic complete-metric holonomy need not admit a global flat
    71	factorization.
    72	
    73	Thus the foundation derives endpoint composition in **abstract depth space**, not a parallel
    74	endpoint endomorphism on the complete physical geometry.
    75	
    76	## 4. Why the exact path groupoid is not a path-ontology theorem
    77	
    78	For a supplied complete metric, ordered pair frames, connecting paths, vertical pair resets, and an
    79	additive depth cocycle, the existing path-groupoid construction composes exactly for every real
    80	`lambda`. That proves mathematical consistency of a path-labelled realization.
    81	
    82	It does not prove that the path label is physical. The founding sources do not say that the
    83	connecting curve, its homotopy class, or its transport history belongs to the positional comparison.
    84	The construction begins only after path input is supplied.
    85	
    86	The round cut-locus control makes the distinction exact: scalar distance at the antipode is single
    87	valued while full frame transports form a path family. Hence path multiplicity in the angular frame
    88	does not logically force the basic scalar clock-depth channel to be path-labelled.
    89	
    90	## 5. Observer-frame reciprocity does not close the fork
    91	
    92	The current owner meaning of observer-frame reciprocity is equality of law without a preferred
    93	macroscopic observer. It requires covariant transformation of any supplied comparison. It does not
    94	require:
    95	
    96	- one global frame or pair section;
    97	- path independence;
    98	- a flat connection or zero holonomy;
    99	- identical numerical components for all observers; or
   100	- path labels to be observable.
   101	
   102	Both an endpoint-only covariant object and a covariant path-groupoid object can satisfy this
   103	principle. Covariance therefore preserves the fork rather than selecting one branch.
   104	
   105	## 6. Consequence for `lambda`
   106	
   107	The global bundle result remains exactly two-tiered:
   108	
   109	```text
   110	typed retained-path meaning: every real constant lambda survives;
   111	parallel endpoint-only meaning on the two S3 controls: lambda=1 only.
   112	```
   113	
   114	Because endpoint-only physical comparison is not derived, the second line cannot select `lambda=1`.
   115	Because path-labelled physical comparison is also not derived, the first line cannot be called the
   116	physical ontology of UDT. It is the complete conditional pathwise realization presently available.
   117	
   118	## 7. Exact source boundary
   119	
   120	The foundation currently reaches this far:
   121	
   122	```text
   123	clock/ruler reciprocal channels
   124	 -> ordered relational depth character
   125	 -> exact abstract comparison, composition, inverse, identity
   126	 -> local clock/ruler readout under its recorded premise.
   127	```
   128	
   129	The next arrow is missing:
   130	
   131	```text
   132	abstract ordered depth
   133	 -?-> complete observer/event/path comparison datum.
   134	```
   135	
   136	No action, field equation, carrier, boundary, density, mass, `X_max`, or dynamics is needed to state
   137	that missing join, and none was activated in this audit.

    53	## What section-free really means
    54	
    55	Section-free does not mean observer-independent. A query law may legitimately answer a question
    56	about a supplied observer/ruler pair and transform naturally when the pair changes. It is not one
    57	tensor at the base event.
    58	
    59	Conversely, an ambient law can be independent of the pair and still leave observer-pair readouts as
    60	downstream query observables. Therefore the absence of a universal natural `N` does not block every
    61	possible native metric law.

    73	plane or establish physical zero mixing.
